Um… it’s usually at least once a week, I think. Mostly at night after work. I’ll stop somewhere and buy way too much food, and then I just… eat all of it in one stretch.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah… I came in because after these eating episodes I’ve been having, I just feel really gross and disappointed in myself. Like I should have more control than that, you know?\n\nAnd then I’ll end up writing these awful things about myself in my journal. I started journaling because someone told me it was supposed to help, and I even bought this nice notebook from the dollar store—well, not nice nice, but it has flowers on it—and now sometimes it just turns into me being really mean to myself after I eat like that. Like something quick, because I’m tired and my feet hurt, and the fluorescent lights in those stores always make everything look kind of… I don’t know, too bright.\n\nBut then I get back to my room and it turns into way more than I planned. Like I’ll have a big bowl of ramen, and then several rice balls, and fried chicken, and some sweets too. And it’s not spread out over the whole night or anything—it’s usually within like an hour or two. I’ll look at all the wrappers after and just feel like, why did I do that?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s the part that scares me. Once I start, it feels like my hand just keeps going to the next thing almost by itself.\n\nLike I’ll be sitting there thinking, Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish, or you need to wash your work shirt for tomorrow, and then I’m still opening the next package anyway. I used to draw little characters at night, just pencil stuff, nothing amazing, but I keep telling myself I’ll do that instead and then… I don’t. I usually don’t stop until the food is gone, or until I feel so uncomfortable that I kind of have to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… really guilty. That’s kind of why I finally came in, honestly. Afterward I just feel gross and disappointed in myself, like, why couldn’t I just stop? Like I should have more control than that.\n\nAnd then I’ll write in my journal, which was supposed to be this helpful thing, but sometimes I just end up writing really awful stuff about myself in there. I don’t even always want to look at it later. It’s embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I eat really fast during those times. Like, I’ll be standing at the little counter in my room, or sitting on my bed, and my sketchbook will still be open next to me like I was supposed to be drawing, but I’m just… eating.\n\nIt’s not even like I’m enjoying it properly. I barely taste it sometimes. I think I’m just trying to get it down before I can think too much, if that makes sense. Like if I slow down, then I’ll realize what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these eating episodes by doing things like vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know how to answer that exactly. I mean, I’ve felt like I should “fix it” afterward, but… I’m not sure about those things you listed.\n\nSometimes I’ll think, like, tomorrow I’ll be better, or I’ll clean my room and get everything back in order—my room gets so cluttered with work stuff and old sketch pages, and then I feel like if the room looks normal maybe I’ll feel normal too. I mean, I’ve felt sick after, like emotionally and sometimes just uncomfortable, but I don’t, like, go and make myself throw up on purpose.\n\nSorry, I’m trying to answer directly. I don’t really keep track of my weight that closely, and I kind of avoid thinking about it too much.\n\nLike, I notice how my clothes feel sometimes, but then I also think maybe they shrank in the laundry, or I’ve just been wearing the same work pants forever because they’re easy to clean in. I have this one pair that has bleach spots all over them from work, and I keep meaning to replace them, but then I don’t, because shopping for clothes is… not my favorite thing.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t noticed a huge clear change, or at least I don’t know if it’s because of the eating episodes. I’m sorry, that’s probably not a very exact answer.\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s usually at least once a week, I think. Mostly at night after work. I’ll stop somewhere and buy way too much food, and then I just… eat all of it in one stretch.\n\nIt’s been like that for the past several months. I can’t really think of a recent month where it went away for more than maybe a week or so. I mean, some weeks are different depending on my schedule, and if I’m really tired after cleaning floors or whatever I’ll tell myself I’m just getting dinner, but… yeah, it usually happens at least one night a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s the part that scares me a little. Once I start, it’s like I’m not really deciding each bite anymore. I just keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically.\n\nAnd I’ll be thinking, like, “Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,” because I do these little drawings and I’ll leave my sketchbook out like I’m going to work on it after dinner, but then I don’t. I just keep eating until the food is gone, or until I feel so uncomfortable that I kind of have to stop. Once I start, it feels like my hand just keeps going to the next thing almost by itself.\n\nLike I’ll be sitting there thinking, Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish, or you need to wash your work shirt for tomorrow, and then I’m still opening the next package anyway. I used to draw little characters at night, just pencil stuff, nothing amazing, but I keep telling myself I’ll do that instead and then… I don’t. I usually don’t stop until the food is gone, or until I feel so uncomfortable that I kind of have to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… really guilty. That’s kind of why I finally came in, honestly. Afterward I just feel gross and disappointed in myself, like, why couldn’t I just stop? Like I should have more control than that.\n\nAnd then I’ll write in my journal, which was supposed to be this helpful thing, but sometimes I just end up writing really awful stuff about myself in there. I don’t even always want to look at it later. It’s embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I eat really fast during those times. Like, I’ll be standing at the little counter in my room, or sitting on my bed, and my sketchbook will still be open next to me like I was supposed to be drawing, but I’m just… eating.\n\nIt’s not even like I’m enjoying it properly. I barely taste it sometimes. I think I’m just trying to get it down before I can think too much, if that makes sense. Like if I slow down, then I’ll realize what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these eating episodes by doing things like vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know how to answer that exactly. I mean, I’ve felt like I should “fix it” afterward, but… I’m not sure about those things you listed.\n\nSometimes I’ll think, like, tomorrow I’ll be better, or I’ll clean my room and get everything back in order—my room gets so cluttered with work stuff and old sketch pages, and then I feel like if the room looks normal maybe I’ll feel normal too. But that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess… I don’t think I have a clear thing I do to make up for it. Not that I can point to, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I… don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt sick after, like emotionally and sometimes just uncomfortable, but I don’t, like, go and make myself throw up on purpose.\n\nSorry, I’m trying to answer directly. My brain keeps going to this one time I cleaned the bathroom at work after someone had gotten sick, and it made me think about how much I hate even being around that, so… no, I don’t think that’s something I do.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I don’t really keep track of my weight that closely, and I kind of avoid thinking about it too much.\n\nLike, I notice how my clothes feel sometimes, but then I also think maybe they shrank in the laundry, or I’ve just been wearing the same work pants forever because they’re easy to clean in. I have this one pair that has bleach spots all over them from work, and I keep meaning to replace them, but then I don’t, because shopping for clothes is… not my favorite thing.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t noticed a huge clear change, or at least I don’t know if it’s because of the eating episodes. I’m sorry, that’s probably not a very exact answer.\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s usually at least once a week, I think. Mostly at night after work. I’ll stop somewhere and buy way too much food, and then I just… eat all of it in one stretch.\n\nIt’s been like that for the past several months. I can’t really think of a recent month where it went away for more than maybe a week or so. I mean, some weeks are different depending on my schedule, and if I’m really tired after cleaning floors or whatever I’ll tell myself I’m just getting dinner, but… yeah, it usually happens at least one night a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s the part that scares me a little. Once I start, it’s like I’m not really deciding each bite anymore. I just keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically.\n\nAnd I’ll be thinking, like, “Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,” because I do these little drawings and I’ll leave my sketchbook out like I’m going to work on it after dinner, but then I don’t. I just keep eating until the food is gone, or until I feel so uncomfortable that I kind of have to stop. So… no, I don’t feel very in control when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… laxatives? I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve seen them in the store, like near the stomach stuff and vitamins, but I don’t really buy that kind of thing.\n\nSorry, my brain went to this one aisle at the pharmacy near my apartment because the lights there buzz really loudly and I always notice it after work. But… no, not that I can think of. I don’t use laxatives after eating.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

Like, I’ll be standing at the little counter in my room, or sitting on my bed, and my sketchbook will still be open next to me like I was supposed to be drawing, but I’m just… eating.\n\nIt’s not even like I’m enjoying it properly. I barely taste it sometimes. I think I’m just trying to get it down before I can think too much, if that makes sense. Like if I slow down, then I’ll realize what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these eating episodes by doing things like vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know how to answer that exactly. I mean, I’ve felt like I should “fix it” afterward, but… I’m not sure about those things you listed.\n\nSometimes I’ll think, like, tomorrow I’ll be better, or I’ll clean my room and get everything back in order—my room gets so cluttered with work stuff and old sketch pages, and then I feel like if the room looks normal maybe I’ll feel normal too. But that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess… I don’t think I have a clear thing I do to make up for it. Not that I can point to, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I… don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt sick after, like emotionally and sometimes just uncomfortable, but I don’t, like, go and make myself throw up on purpose.\n\nSorry, I’m trying to answer directly. My brain keeps going to this one time I cleaned the bathroom at work after someone had gotten sick, and it made me think about how much I hate even being around that, so… no, I don’t think that’s something I do.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I don’t really keep track of my weight that closely, and I kind of avoid thinking about it too much.\n\nLike, I notice how my clothes feel sometimes, but then I also think maybe they shrank in the laundry, or I’ve just been wearing the same work pants forever because they’re easy to clean in. I have this one pair that has bleach spots all over them from work, and I keep meaning to replace them, but then I don’t, because shopping for clothes is… not my favorite thing.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t noticed a huge clear change, or at least I don’t know if it’s because of the eating episodes. I’m sorry, that’s probably not a very exact answer.\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s usually at least once a week, I think. Mostly at night after work. I’ll stop somewhere and buy way too much food, and then I just… eat all of it in one stretch.\n\nIt’s been like that for the past several months. I can’t really think of a recent month where it went away for more than maybe a week or so. I mean, some weeks are different depending on my schedule, and if I’m really tired after cleaning floors or whatever I’ll tell myself I’m just getting dinner, but… yeah, it usually happens at least one night a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s the part that scares me a little. Once I start, it’s like I’m not really deciding each bite anymore. I just keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically.\n\nAnd I’ll be thinking, like, “Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,” because I do these little drawings and I’ll leave my sketchbook out like I’m going to work on it after dinner, but then I don’t. I just keep eating until the food is gone, or until I feel so uncomfortable that I kind of have to stop. So… no, I don’t feel very in control when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… laxatives? I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve seen them in the store, like near the stomach stuff and vitamins, but I don’t really buy that kind of thing.\n\nSorry, my brain went to this one aisle at the pharmacy near my apartment because the lights there buzz really loudly and I always notice it after work. But… no, not that I can think of. I don’t use laxatives after eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so? I mean, my job is pretty physical already, like I’m on my feet a lot cleaning, and sometimes after a shift my legs are just done. \n\nAfter those nights I’m usually not thinking, “I need to go work this off” or anything like that. I more just end up sitting there feeling gross and tired. I guess sometimes I tell myself I should walk more, like there’s this park near my bus stop that I always say I’m going to go around once, but then I usually just go home. So… no, I wouldn’t say I exercise excessively after.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Like, I’ll be standing at the little counter in my room, or sitting on my bed, and my sketchbook will still be open next to me like I was supposed to be drawing, but I’m just… eating.\n\nIt’s not even like I’m enjoying it properly. I barely taste it sometimes. I think I’m just trying to get it down before I can think too much, if that makes sense. Like if I slow down, then I’ll realize what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these eating episodes by doing things like vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know how to answer that exactly. I mean, I’ve felt like I should “fix it” afterward, but… I’m not sure about those things you listed.\n\nSometimes I’ll think, like, tomorrow I’ll be better, or I’ll clean my room and get everything back in order—my room gets so cluttered with work stuff and old sketch pages, and then I feel like if the room looks normal maybe I’ll feel normal too. But that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess… I don’t think I have a clear thing I do to make up for it. Not that I can point to, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I… don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt sick after, like emotionally and sometimes just uncomfortable, but I don’t, like, go and make myself throw up on purpose.\n\nSorry, I’m trying to answer directly. My brain keeps going to this one time I cleaned the bathroom at work after someone had gotten sick, and it made me think about how much I hate even being around that, so… no, I don’t think that’s something I do.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I don’t really keep track of my weight that closely, and I kind of avoid thinking about it too much.\n\nLike, I notice how my clothes feel sometimes, but then I also think maybe they shrank in the laundry, or I’ve just been wearing the same work pants forever because they’re easy to clean in. I have this one pair that has bleach spots all over them from work, and I keep meaning to replace them, but then I don’t, because shopping for clothes is… not my favorite thing.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t noticed a huge clear change, or at least I don’t know if it’s because of the eating episodes. I’m sorry, that’s probably not a very exact answer.\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s usually at least once a week, I think. Mostly at night after work. I’ll stop somewhere and buy way too much food, and then I just… eat all of it in one stretch.\n\nIt’s been like that for the past several months. I can’t really think of a recent month where it went away for more than maybe a week or so. I mean, some weeks are different depending on my schedule, and if I’m really tired after cleaning floors or whatever I’ll tell myself I’m just getting dinner, but… yeah, it usually happens at least one night a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s the part that scares me a little. Once I start, it’s like I’m not really deciding each bite anymore. I just keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically.\n\nAnd I’ll be thinking, like, “Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,” because I do these little drawings and I’ll leave my sketchbook out like I’m going to work on it after dinner, but then I don’t. I just keep eating until the food is gone, or until I feel so uncomfortable that I kind of have to stop. So… no, I don’t feel very in control when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… laxatives? I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve seen them in the store, like near the stomach stuff and vitamins, but I don’t really buy that kind of thing.\n\nSorry, my brain went to this one aisle at the pharmacy near my apartment because the lights there buzz really loudly and I always notice it after work. But… no, not that I can think of. I don’t use laxatives after eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so? I mean, my job is pretty physical already, like I’m on my feet a lot cleaning, and sometimes after a shift my legs are just done. \n\nAfter those nights I’m usually not thinking, “I need to go work this off” or anything like that. I more just end up sitting there feeling gross and tired. I guess sometimes I tell myself I should walk more, like there’s this park near my bus stop that I always say I’m going to go around once, but then I usually just go home. So… no, I wouldn’t say I exercise excessively after.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any noticeable weight gain or loss over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I don’t weigh myself that often, honestly. There’s a scale in the staff bathroom at work, I think, but I try not to look at it because someone stuck this weird little sticker on it ages ago and now that’s what I notice every time.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel… kind of the same? Maybe some days tighter, but I don’t know if that’s actual weight or just, like, laundry shrinking stuff or me being bloated. So I haven’t noticed a big gain or loss, no.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’ll look at all the wrappers after and just feel like, why did I do that?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s the part that scares me. Once I start, it feels like my hand just keeps going to the next thing almost by itself.\n\nLike I’ll be sitting there thinking, Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish, or you need to wash your work shirt for tomorrow, and then I’m still opening the next package anyway. I used to draw little characters at night, just pencil stuff, nothing amazing, but I keep telling myself I’ll do that instead and then… I don’t. I usually don’t stop until the food is gone, or until I feel so uncomfortable that I kind of have to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… really guilty. That’s kind of why I finally came in, honestly. Afterward I just feel gross and disappointed in myself, like, why couldn’t I just stop? Like I should have more control than that.\n\nAnd then I’ll write in my journal, which was supposed to be this helpful thing, but sometimes I just end up writing really awful stuff about myself in there. I don’t even always want to look at it later. It’s embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I eat really fast during those times. Like, I’ll be standing at the little counter in my room, or sitting on my bed, and my sketchbook will still be open next to me like I was supposed to be drawing, but I’m just… eating.\n\nIt’s not even like I’m enjoying it properly. I barely taste it sometimes. I think I’m just trying to get it down before I can think too much, if that makes sense. Like if I slow down, then I’ll realize what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these eating episodes by doing things like vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know how to answer that exactly. I mean, I’ve felt like I should “fix it” afterward, but… I’m not sure about those things you listed.\n\nSometimes I’ll think, like, tomorrow I’ll be better, or I’ll clean my room and get everything back in order—my room gets so cluttered with work stuff and old sketch pages, and then I feel like if the room looks normal maybe I’ll feel normal too. But that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess… I don’t think I have a clear thing I do to make up for it. Not that I can point to, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I… don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt sick after, like emotionally and sometimes just uncomfortable, but I don’t, like, go and make myself throw up on purpose.\n\nSorry, I’m trying to answer directly. My brain keeps going to this one time I cleaned the bathroom at work after someone had gotten sick, and it made me think about how much I hate even being around that, so… no, I don’t think that’s something I do.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I don’t really keep track of my weight that closely, and I kind of avoid thinking about it too much.\n\nLike, I notice how my clothes feel sometimes, but then I also think maybe they shrank in the laundry, or I’ve just been wearing the same work pants forever because they’re easy to clean in. I have this one pair that has bleach spots all over them from work, and I keep meaning to replace them, but then I don’t, because shopping for clothes is… not my favorite thing.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t noticed a huge clear change, or at least I don’t know if it’s because of the eating episodes. I’m sorry, that’s probably not a very exact answer.\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s usually at least once a week, I think. Mostly at night after work. I’ll stop somewhere and buy way too much food, and then I just… eat all of it in one stretch.\n\nIt’s been like that for the past several months. I can’t really think of a recent month where it went away for more than maybe a week or so. I mean, some weeks are different depending on my schedule, and if I’m really tired after cleaning floors or whatever I’ll tell myself I’m just getting dinner, but… yeah, it usually happens at least one night a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s the part that scares me a little. Once I start, it’s like I’m not really deciding each bite anymore. I just keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically.\n\nAnd I’ll be thinking, like, “Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,” because I do these little drawings and I’ll leave my sketchbook out like I’m going to work on it after dinner, but then I don’t. I just keep eating until the food is gone, or until I feel so uncomfortable that I kind of have to stop. So… no, I don’t feel very in control when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… laxatives? I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve seen them in the store, like near the stomach stuff and vitamins, but I don’t really buy that kind of thing.\n\nSorry, my brain went to this one aisle at the pharmacy near my apartment because the lights there buzz really loudly and I always notice it after work. But… no, not that I can think of. I don’t use laxatives after eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so? I mean, my job is pretty physical already, like I’m on my feet a lot cleaning, and sometimes after a shift my legs are just done. \n\nAfter those nights I’m usually not thinking, “I need to go work this off” or anything like that. I more just end up sitting there feeling gross and tired. I guess sometimes I tell myself I should walk more, like there’s this park near my bus stop that I always say I’m going to go around once, but then I usually just go home. So… no, I wouldn’t say I exercise excessively after.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any noticeable weight gain or loss over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I don’t weigh myself that often, honestly. There’s a scale in the staff bathroom at work, I think, but I try not to look at it because someone stuck this weird little sticker on it ages ago and now that’s what I notice every time.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel… kind of the same? Maybe some days tighter, but I don’t know if that’s actual weight or just, like, laundry shrinking stuff or me being bloated. So I haven’t noticed a big gain or loss, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or severely restrict your food intake after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I fast, no. Like, sometimes the next morning I don’t really feel hungry, or I’m running late and just have coffee or something, but it’s not like I’m making a plan to not eat all day.\n\nI did try once to be like, “Okay, I’m only going to eat salads this week,” but then I got annoyed because the lettuce at the store near me always gets slimy so fast, and I hate wasting money on food I don’t end up using. Sorry—um, no, I wouldn’t say I severely restrict after. I mostly just feel bad and then try to go back to normal.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I can’t really think of a recent month where it went away for more than maybe a week or so. I mean, some weeks are different depending on my schedule, and if I’m really tired after cleaning floors or whatever I’ll tell myself I’m just getting dinner, but… yeah, it usually happens at least one night a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s the part that scares me a little. But… no, not that I can think of. I don’t use laxatives after eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so? I more just end up sitting there feeling gross and tired. So… no, I wouldn’t say I exercise excessively after.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any noticeable weight gain or loss over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I don’t weigh myself that often, honestly. Maybe some days tighter, but I don’t know if that’s actual weight or just, like, laundry shrinking stuff or me being bloated. So I haven’t noticed a big gain or loss, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or severely restrict your food intake after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I fast, no. Like, sometimes the next morning I don’t really feel hungry, or I’m running late and just have coffee or something, but it’s not like I’m making a plan to not eat all day.\n\nI did try once to be like, “Okay, I’m only going to eat salads this week,” but then I got annoyed because the lettuce at the store near me always gets slimy so fast, and I hate wasting money on food I don’t end up using. Sorry—um, no, I wouldn’t say I severely restrict after. I mostly just feel bad and then try to go back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever exercised excessively in the past as a way to make up for eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. Like I should have more control than that, you know?\n\nAnd then I’ll end up writing these awful things about myself in my journal. I started journaling because someone told me it was supposed to help, and I even bought this nice notebook from the dollar store—well, not nice nice, but it has flowers on it—and now sometimes it just turns into me being really mean to myself after I eat like that. It’s been bothering me enough that I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by these 'eating episodes' you've been having?\n\nPatient: It’s like… some nights after my cleaning shift, I’ll just mean to stop at the convenience store for dinner. Like something quick, because I’m tired and my feet hurt, and the fluorescent lights in those stores always make everything look kind of… I don’t know, too bright.\n\nBut then I get back to my room and it turns into way more than I planned. Like I’ll have a big bowl of ramen, and then several rice balls, and fried chicken, and some sweets too. And it’s not spread out over the whole night or anything—it’s usually within like an hour or two. I’ll look at all the wrappers after and just feel like, why did I do that?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s the part that scares me. Once I start, it feels like my hand just keeps going to the next thing almost by itself.\n\nLike I’ll be sitting there thinking, Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish, or you need to wash your work shirt for tomorrow, and then I’m still opening the next package anyway. I used to draw little characters at night, just pencil stuff, nothing amazing, but I keep telling myself I’ll do that instead and then… I don’t. I usually don’t stop until the food is gone, or until I feel so uncomfortable that I kind of have to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… really guilty. That’s kind of why I finally came in, honestly. Afterward I just feel gross and disappointed in myself, like, why couldn’t I just stop? Like I should have more control than that.\n\nAnd then I’ll write in my journal, which was supposed to be this helpful thing, but sometimes I just end up writing really awful stuff about myself in there. I don’t even always want to look at it later. It’s embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I eat really fast during those times. Like, I’ll be standing at the little counter in my room, or sitting on my bed, and my sketchbook will still be open next to me like I was supposed to be drawing, but I’m just… eating.\n\nIt’s not even like I’m enjoying it properly. I barely taste it sometimes. I think I’m just trying to get it down before I can think too much, if that makes sense. Like if I slow down, then I’ll realize what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these eating episodes by doing things like vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know how to answer that exactly. I mean, I’ve felt like I should “fix it” afterward, but… I’m not sure about those things you listed.\n\nSometimes I’ll think, like, tomorrow I’ll be better, or I’ll clean my room and get everything back in order—my room gets so cluttered with work stuff and old sketch pages, and then I feel like if the room looks normal maybe I’ll feel normal too. But that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess… I don’t think I have a clear thing I do to make up for it. Not that I can point to, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I… don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt sick after, like emotionally and sometimes just uncomfortable, but I don’t, like, go and make myself throw up on purpose.\n\nSorry, I’m trying to answer directly. My brain keeps going to this one time I cleaned the bathroom at work after someone had gotten sick, and it made me think about how much I hate even being around that, so… no, I don’t think that’s something I do.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I don’t really keep track of my weight that closely, and I kind of avoid thinking about it too much.\n\nLike, I notice how my clothes feel sometimes, but then I also think maybe they shrank in the laundry, or I’ve just been wearing the same work pants forever because they’re easy to clean in. I have this one pair that has bleach spots all over them from work, and I keep meaning to replace them, but then I don’t, because shopping for clothes is… not my favorite thing.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t noticed a huge clear change, or at least I don’t know if it’s because of the eating episodes. I’m sorry, that’s probably not a very exact answer.\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s usually at least once a week, I think. Mostly at night after work. I’ll stop somewhere and buy way too much food, and then I just… eat all of it in one stretch.\n\nIt’s been like that for the past several months. I can’t really think of a recent month where it went away for more than maybe a week or so. I mean, some weeks are different depending on my schedule, and if I’m really tired after cleaning floors or whatever I’ll tell myself I’m just getting dinner, but… yeah, it usually happens at least one night a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s the part that scares me a little. Once I start, it’s like I’m not really deciding each bite anymore. I just keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically.\n\nAnd I’ll be thinking, like, “Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,” because I do these little drawings and I’ll leave my sketchbook out like I’m going to work on it after dinner, but then I don’t. I just keep eating until the food is gone, or until I feel so uncomfortable that I kind of have to stop. So… no, I don’t feel very in control when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… laxatives? I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve seen them in the store, like near the stomach stuff and vitamins, but I don’t really buy that kind of thing.\n\nSorry, my brain went to this one aisle at the pharmacy near my apartment because the lights there buzz really loudly and I always notice it after work. But… no, not that I can think of. I don’t use laxatives after eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so? I mean, my job is pretty physical already, like I’m on my feet a lot cleaning, and sometimes after a shift my legs are just done. \n\nAfter those nights I’m usually not thinking, “I need to go work this off” or anything like that. I more just end up sitting there feeling gross and tired. I guess sometimes I tell myself I should walk more, like there’s this park near my bus stop that I always say I’m going to go around once, but then I usually just go home. So… no, I wouldn’t say I exercise excessively after.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any noticeable weight gain or loss over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I don’t weigh myself that often, honestly. There’s a scale in the staff bathroom at work, I think, but I try not to look at it because someone stuck this weird little sticker on it ages ago and now that’s what I notice every time.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel… kind of the same? Maybe some days tighter, but I don’t know if that’s actual weight or just, like, laundry shrinking stuff or me being bloated. So I haven’t noticed a big gain or loss, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or severely restrict your food intake after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I fast, no. Like, sometimes the next morning I don’t really feel hungry, or I’m running late and just have coffee or something, but it’s not like I’m making a plan to not eat all day.\n\nI did try once to be like, “Okay, I’m only going to eat salads this week,” but then I got annoyed because the lettuce at the store near me always gets slimy so fast, and I hate wasting money on food I don’t end up using. Sorry—um, no, I wouldn’t say I severely restrict after. I mostly just feel bad and then try to go back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever exercised excessively in the past as a way to make up for eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. Like, in the past I’ve had times where I’d tell myself I should “get healthier” and I’d download some step-counting app or whatever, but then I’d forget about it after two days because my phone battery is already terrible and those apps always seem to drain it.\n\nBut as far as exercising to make up for eating… no, I don’t think I’ve done that. I mean, I’ve felt guilty and thought I should move more, but not like going to the gym for hours or punishing myself with workouts or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use any methods to try to compensate for the calories you consumed during these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really? I’m trying to think what counts as “methods.” Like I don’t take anything or do some whole routine after, if that’s what you mean.\n\nUsually I just feel gross about it and tell myself I’ll be more normal the next day, and then I get distracted by work or whatever. Sometimes after cleaning the stairwells at my job I’m already so sweaty that I’m like, well, that was exercise, I guess—but I’m not doing it on purpose to cancel anything out. So… no, I don’t think I really compensate like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever engage in any behaviors to try to prevent weight gain after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I worry about it, like I’ll stand there and be annoyed at myself, but I don’t really do a specific thing after to stop gaining weight.\n\nSometimes I’ll tell myself I’m going to “be good” the next day, but then the next day is just… work, and I’m scrubbing bathrooms or taking trash out and trying not to get bleach on my shoes. So it’s more like guilty thoughts than an actual behavior, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Like, in the past I’ve had times where I’d tell myself I should “get healthier” and I’d download some step-counting app or whatever, but then I’d forget about it after two days because my phone battery is already terrible and those apps always seem to drain it.\n\nBut as far as exercising to make up for eating… no, I don’t think I’ve done that. I mean, I’ve felt guilty and thought I should move more, but not like going to the gym for hours or punishing myself with workouts or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use any methods to try to compensate for the calories you consumed during these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really? I’m trying to think what counts as “methods.” Like I don’t take anything or do some whole routine after, if that’s what you mean.\n\nUsually I just feel gross about it and tell myself I’ll be more normal the next day, and then I get distracted by work or whatever. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think so… I mean, “undo” makes me think of like, some specific thing you’re supposed to do after, and I don’t really have that.

Mostly I just feel bad and kind of avoid thinking about it. Or I’ll tell myself, okay, tomorrow I’ll get back on track, but then tomorrow I’m just tired and dealing with whatever’s going on at work. Like yesterday one of the soap dispensers leaked everywhere and it was this whole thing—sorry, that’s not really the point.

But no, I don’t think I really do anything to undo it.

It’s been bothering me enough that I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by these 'eating episodes' you've been having?\n\nPatient: It’s like… some nights after my cleaning shift, I’ll just mean to stop at the convenience store for dinner. Like something quick, because I’m tired and my feet hurt, and the fluorescent lights in those stores always make everything look kind of… I don’t know, too bright.\n\nBut then I get back to my room and it turns into way more than I planned. Like I’ll have a big bowl of ramen, and then several rice balls, and fried chicken, and some sweets too. I’ll look at all the wrappers after and just feel like, why did I do that?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s the part that scares me. Once I start, it feels like my hand just keeps going to the next thing almost by itself.\n\nLike I’ll be sitting there thinking, Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish, or you need to wash your work shirt for tomorrow, and then I’m still opening the next package anyway. I used to draw little characters at night, just pencil stuff, nothing amazing, but I keep telling myself I’ll do that instead and then… I don’t. I usually don’t stop until the food is gone, or until I feel so uncomfortable that I kind of have to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… really guilty. I eat really fast during those times. Like, I’ll be standing at the little counter in my room, or sitting on my bed, and my sketchbook will still be open next to me like I was supposed to be drawing, but I’m just… eating.\n\nIt’s not even like I’m enjoying it properly. I barely taste it sometimes. I think I’m just trying to get it down before I can think too much, if that makes sense. Like if I slow down, then I’ll realize what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these eating episodes by doing things like vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know how to answer that exactly. But that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess… I don’t think I have a clear thing I do to make up for it. Not that I can point to, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I… don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt sick after, like emotionally and sometimes just uncomfortable, but I don’t, like, go and make myself throw up on purpose.\n\nSorry, I’m trying to answer directly. My brain keeps going to this one time I cleaned the bathroom at work after someone had gotten sick, and it made me think about how much I hate even being around that, so… no, I don’t think that’s something I do.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I don’t really keep track of my weight that closely, and I kind of avoid thinking about it too much.\n\nLike, I notice how my clothes feel sometimes, but then I also think maybe they shrank in the laundry, or I’ve just been wearing the same work pants forever because they’re easy to clean in. I have this one pair that has bleach spots all over them from work, and I keep meaning to replace them, but then I don’t, because shopping for clothes is… not my favorite thing.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t noticed a huge clear change, or at least I don’t know if it’s because of the eating episodes. I’m sorry, that’s probably not a very exact answer.\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s usually at least once a week, I think. Mostly at night after work. I’ll stop somewhere and buy way too much food, and then I just… eat all of it in one stretch.\n\nIt’s been like that for the past several months. I can’t really think of a recent month where it went away for more than maybe a week or so. I mean, some weeks are different depending on my schedule, and if I’m really tired after cleaning floors or whatever I’ll tell myself I’m just getting dinner, but… yeah, it usually happens at least one night a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s the part that scares me a little. Once I start, it’s like I’m not really deciding each bite anymore. I just keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically.\n\nAnd I’ll be thinking, like, “Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,” because I do these little drawings and I’ll leave my sketchbook out like I’m going to work on it after dinner, but then I don’t. I just keep eating until the food is gone, or until I feel so uncomfortable that I kind of have to stop. So… no, I don’t feel very in control when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… laxatives? I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve seen them in the store, like near the stomach stuff and vitamins, but I don’t really buy that kind of thing.\n\nSorry, my brain went to this one aisle at the pharmacy near my apartment because the lights there buzz really loudly and I always notice it after work. But… no, not that I can think of. I don’t use laxatives after eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so? I more just end up sitting there feeling gross and tired. I guess sometimes I tell myself I should walk more, like there’s this park near my bus stop that I always say I’m going to go around once, but then I usually just go home. So… no, I wouldn’t say I exercise excessively after.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any noticeable weight gain or loss over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I don’t weigh myself that often, honestly. There’s a scale in the staff bathroom at work, I think, but I try not to look at it because someone stuck this weird little sticker on it ages ago and now that’s what I notice every time.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel… kind of the same? Maybe some days tighter, but I don’t know if that’s actual weight or just, like, laundry shrinking stuff or me being bloated. So I haven’t noticed a big gain or loss, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or severely restrict your food intake after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I fast, no. Like, sometimes the next morning I don’t really feel hungry, or I’m running late and just have coffee or something, but it’s not like I’m making a plan to not eat all day.\n\nI did try once to be like, “Okay, I’m only going to eat salads this week,” but then I got annoyed because the lettuce at the store near me always gets slimy so fast, and I hate wasting money on food I don’t end up using. Yeah… I guess that does happen. Not every single night, but on some nights after my cleaning shift I’ll stop at the convenience store just meaning to get dinner, and then I end up buying way more.

And then I don’t really do anything after to make up for it, like I said… I just sort of sit with it and feel bad, or try to distract myself.

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.8, F50.4\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah… I came in because after these eating episodes I’ve been having, I just feel really gross and disappointed in myself. Like I should have more control than that, you know?\n\nAnd then I’ll end up writing these awful things about myself in my journal. I started journaling because someone told me it was supposed to help, and I even bought this nice notebook from the dollar store—well, not nice nice, but it has flowers on it—and now sometimes it just turns into me being really mean to myself after I eat like that. It’s been bothering me enough that I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by these 'eating episodes' you've been having?\n\nPatient: It’s like… some nights after my cleaning shift, I’ll just mean to stop at the convenience store for dinner. Like something quick, because I’m tired and my feet hurt, and the fluorescent lights in those stores always make everything look kind of… I don’t know, too bright.\n\nBut then I get back to my room and it turns into way more than I planned. Like I’ll have a big bowl of ramen, and then several rice balls, and fried chicken, and some sweets too. And it’s not spread out over the whole night or anything—it’s usually within like an hour or two. I’ll look at all the wrappers after and just feel like, why did I do that?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s the part that scares me. Once I start, it feels like my hand just keeps going to the next thing almost by itself.\n\nLike I’ll be sitting there thinking, Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish, or you need to wash your work shirt for tomorrow, and then I’m still opening the next package anyway. I used to draw little characters at night, just pencil stuff, nothing amazing, but I keep telling myself I’ll do that instead and then… I don’t. I usually don’t stop until the food is gone, or until I feel so uncomfortable that I kind of have to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… really guilty. That’s kind of why I finally came in, honestly. Afterward I just feel gross and disappointed in myself, like, why couldn’t I just stop? Like I should have more control than that.\n\nAnd then I’ll write in my journal, which was supposed to be this helpful thing, but sometimes I just end up writing really awful stuff about myself in there. I don’t even always want to look at it later. It’s embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I eat really fast during those times. Like, I’ll be standing at the little counter in my room, or sitting on my bed, and my sketchbook will still be open next to me like I was supposed to be drawing, but I’m just… eating.\n\nIt’s not even like I’m enjoying it properly. I barely taste it sometimes. I think I’m just trying to get it down before I can think too much, if that makes sense. Like if I slow down, then I’ll realize what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to make up for these eating episodes by doing things like vomiting, using laxatives, or exercising excessively?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know how to answer that exactly. I mean, I’ve felt like I should “fix it” afterward, but… I’m not sure about those things you listed.\n\nSometimes I’ll think, like, tomorrow I’ll be better, or I’ll clean my room and get everything back in order—my room gets so cluttered with work stuff and old sketch pages, and then I feel like if the room looks normal maybe I’ll feel normal too. But that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess… I don’t think I have a clear thing I do to make up for it. Not that I can point to, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I… don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt sick after, like emotionally and sometimes just uncomfortable, but I don’t, like, go and make myself throw up on purpose.\n\nSorry, I’m trying to answer directly. My brain keeps going to this one time I cleaned the bathroom at work after someone had gotten sick, and it made me think about how much I hate even being around that, so… no, I don’t think that’s something I do.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I don’t really keep track of my weight that closely, and I kind of avoid thinking about it too much.\n\nLike, I notice how my clothes feel sometimes, but then I also think maybe they shrank in the laundry, or I’ve just been wearing the same work pants forever because they’re easy to clean in. I have this one pair that has bleach spots all over them from work, and I keep meaning to replace them, but then I don’t, because shopping for clothes is… not my favorite thing.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t noticed a huge clear change, or at least I don’t know if it’s because of the eating episodes. I’m sorry, that’s probably not a very exact answer.\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s usually at least once a week, I think. Mostly at night after work. I’ll stop somewhere and buy way too much food, and then I just… eat all of it in one stretch.\n\nIt’s been like that for the past several months. I can’t really think of a recent month where it went away for more than maybe a week or so. I mean, some weeks are different depending on my schedule, and if I’m really tired after cleaning floors or whatever I’ll tell myself I’m just getting dinner, but… yeah, it usually happens at least one night a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s the part that scares me a little. Once I start, it’s like I’m not really deciding each bite anymore. I just keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically.\n\nAnd I’ll be thinking, like, “Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,” because I do these little drawings and I’ll leave my sketchbook out like I’m going to work on it after dinner, but then I don’t. I just keep eating until the food is gone, or until I feel so uncomfortable that I kind of have to stop. So… no, I don’t feel very in control when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… laxatives? I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve seen them in the store, like near the stomach stuff and vitamins, but I don’t really buy that kind of thing.\n\nSorry, my brain went to this one aisle at the pharmacy near my apartment because the lights there buzz really loudly and I always notice it after work. But… no, not that I can think of. I don’t use laxatives after eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so? I mean, my job is pretty physical already, like I’m on my feet a lot cleaning, and sometimes after a shift my legs are just done. \n\nAfter those nights I’m usually not thinking, “I need to go work this off” or anything like that. I more just end up sitting there feeling gross and tired. I guess sometimes I tell myself I should walk more, like there’s this park near my bus stop that I always say I’m going to go around once, but then I usually just go home. So… no, I wouldn’t say I exercise excessively after.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any noticeable weight gain or loss over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I don’t weigh myself that often, honestly. There’s a scale in the staff bathroom at work, I think, but I try not to look at it because someone stuck this weird little sticker on it ages ago and now that’s what I notice every time.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel… kind of the same? Maybe some days tighter, but I don’t know if that’s actual weight or just, like, laundry shrinking stuff or me being bloated. So I haven’t noticed a big gain or loss, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or severely restrict your food intake after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I fast, no. Like, sometimes the next morning I don’t really feel hungry, or I’m running late and just have coffee or something, but it’s not like I’m making a plan to not eat all day.\n\nI did try once to be like, “Okay, I’m only going to eat salads this week,” but then I got annoyed because the lettuce at the store near me always gets slimy so fast, and I hate wasting money on food I don’t end up using. Sorry—um, no, I wouldn’t say I severely restrict after. I mostly just feel bad and then try to go back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever exercised excessively in the past as a way to make up for eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. Like, in the past I’ve had times where I’d tell myself I should “get healthier” and I’d download some step-counting app or whatever, but then I’d forget about it after two days because my phone battery is already terrible and those apps always seem to drain it.\n\nBut as far as exercising to make up for eating… no, I don’t think I’ve done that. I mean, I’ve felt guilty and thought I should move more, but not like going to the gym for hours or punishing myself with workouts or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use any methods to try to compensate for the calories you consumed during these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really? I’m trying to think what counts as “methods.” Like I don’t take anything or do some whole routine after, if that’s what you mean.\n\nUsually I just feel gross about it and tell myself I’ll be more normal the next day, and then I get distracted by work or whatever. Sometimes after cleaning the stairwells at my job I’m already so sweaty that I’m like, well, that was exercise, I guess—but I’m not doing it on purpose to cancel anything out. So… no, I don’t think I really compensate like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever engage in any behaviors to try to prevent weight gain after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I worry about it, like I’ll stand there and be annoyed at myself, but I don’t really do a specific thing after to stop gaining weight.\n\nSometimes I’ll tell myself I’m going to “be good” the next day, but then the next day is just… work, and I’m scrubbing bathrooms or taking trash out and trying not to get bleach on my shoes. So it’s more like guilty thoughts than an actual behavior, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever do anything to try to undo the effects of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, “undo” makes me think of like, some specific thing you’re supposed to do after, and I don’t really have that.\n\nMostly I just feel bad and kind of avoid thinking about it. Or I’ll tell myself, okay, tomorrow I’ll get back on track, but then tomorrow I’m just tired and dealing with whatever’s going on at work. Like yesterday one of the soap dispensers leaked everywhere and it was this whole thing—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nBut no, I don’t think I really do anything to undo it.\n\nDoctor: Do you regularly have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short time without trying to make up for it afterwards?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that does happen. Not every single night, but on some nights after my cleaning shift I’ll stop at the convenience store just meaning to get dinner, and then I end up buying way more.\n\nLike I’ll eat a big bowl of ramen, a few rice balls, fried chicken, and then sweets too, all back in my room within maybe an hour or two. It’s kind of embarrassing saying it out loud. And then I don’t really do anything after to make up for it, like I said… I just sort of sit with it and feel bad, or try to distract myself.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

My brain keeps going to this one time I cleaned the bathroom at work after someone had gotten sick, and it made me think about how much I hate even being around that, so… no, I don’t think that’s something I do.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I don’t really keep track of my weight that closely, and I kind of avoid thinking about it too much.\n\nLike, I notice how my clothes feel sometimes, but then I also think maybe they shrank in the laundry, or I’ve just been wearing the same work pants forever because they’re easy to clean in. I have this one pair that has bleach spots all over them from work, and I keep meaning to replace them, but then I don’t, because shopping for clothes is… not my favorite thing.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t noticed a huge clear change, or at least I don’t know if it’s because of the eating episodes. I’m sorry, that’s probably not a very exact answer.\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s usually at least once a week, I think. Mostly at night after work. I’ll stop somewhere and buy way too much food, and then I just… eat all of it in one stretch.\n\nIt’s been like that for the past several months. I can’t really think of a recent month where it went away for more than maybe a week or so. I mean, some weeks are different depending on my schedule, and if I’m really tired after cleaning floors or whatever I’ll tell myself I’m just getting dinner, but… yeah, it usually happens at least one night a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s the part that scares me a little. Once I start, it’s like I’m not really deciding each bite anymore. I just keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically.\n\nAnd I’ll be thinking, like, “Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,” because I do these little drawings and I’ll leave my sketchbook out like I’m going to work on it after dinner, but then I don’t. I just keep eating until the food is gone, or until I feel so uncomfortable that I kind of have to stop. So… no, I don’t feel very in control when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… laxatives? I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve seen them in the store, like near the stomach stuff and vitamins, but I don’t really buy that kind of thing.\n\nSorry, my brain went to this one aisle at the pharmacy near my apartment because the lights there buzz really loudly and I always notice it after work. But… no, not that I can think of. I don’t use laxatives after eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so? I mean, my job is pretty physical already, like I’m on my feet a lot cleaning, and sometimes after a shift my legs are just done. \n\nAfter those nights I’m usually not thinking, “I need to go work this off” or anything like that. I more just end up sitting there feeling gross and tired. I guess sometimes I tell myself I should walk more, like there’s this park near my bus stop that I always say I’m going to go around once, but then I usually just go home. So… no, I wouldn’t say I exercise excessively after.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any noticeable weight gain or loss over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I don’t weigh myself that often, honestly. There’s a scale in the staff bathroom at work, I think, but I try not to look at it because someone stuck this weird little sticker on it ages ago and now that’s what I notice every time.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel… kind of the same? Maybe some days tighter, but I don’t know if that’s actual weight or just, like, laundry shrinking stuff or me being bloated. So I haven’t noticed a big gain or loss, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or severely restrict your food intake after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I fast, no. Like, sometimes the next morning I don’t really feel hungry, or I’m running late and just have coffee or something, but it’s not like I’m making a plan to not eat all day.\n\nI did try once to be like, “Okay, I’m only going to eat salads this week,” but then I got annoyed because the lettuce at the store near me always gets slimy so fast, and I hate wasting money on food I don’t end up using. Sorry—um, no, I wouldn’t say I severely restrict after. I mostly just feel bad and then try to go back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever exercised excessively in the past as a way to make up for eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. Like, in the past I’ve had times where I’d tell myself I should “get healthier” and I’d download some step-counting app or whatever, but then I’d forget about it after two days because my phone battery is already terrible and those apps always seem to drain it.\n\nBut as far as exercising to make up for eating… no, I don’t think I’ve done that. I mean, I’ve felt guilty and thought I should move more, but not like going to the gym for hours or punishing myself with workouts or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use any methods to try to compensate for the calories you consumed during these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really? I’m trying to think what counts as “methods.” Like I don’t take anything or do some whole routine after, if that’s what you mean.\n\nUsually I just feel gross about it and tell myself I’ll be more normal the next day, and then I get distracted by work or whatever. Sometimes after cleaning the stairwells at my job I’m already so sweaty that I’m like, well, that was exercise, I guess—but I’m not doing it on purpose to cancel anything out. So… no, I don’t think I really compensate like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever engage in any behaviors to try to prevent weight gain after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I worry about it, like I’ll stand there and be annoyed at myself, but I don’t really do a specific thing after to stop gaining weight.\n\nSometimes I’ll tell myself I’m going to “be good” the next day, but then the next day is just… work, and I’m scrubbing bathrooms or taking trash out and trying not to get bleach on my shoes. So it’s more like guilty thoughts than an actual behavior, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever do anything to try to undo the effects of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, “undo” makes me think of like, some specific thing you’re supposed to do after, and I don’t really have that.\n\nMostly I just feel bad and kind of avoid thinking about it. Or I’ll tell myself, okay, tomorrow I’ll get back on track, but then tomorrow I’m just tired and dealing with whatever’s going on at work. Like yesterday one of the soap dispensers leaked everywhere and it was this whole thing—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nBut no, I don’t think I really do anything to undo it.\n\nDoctor: Do you regularly have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short time without trying to make up for it afterwards?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that does happen. Not every single night, but on some nights after my cleaning shift I’ll stop at the convenience store just meaning to get dinner, and then I end up buying way more.\n\nLike I’ll eat a big bowl of ramen, a few rice balls, fried chicken, and then sweets too, all back in my room within maybe an hour or two. It’s kind of embarrassing saying it out loud. And then I don’t really do anything after to make up for it, like I said… I just sort of sit with it and feel bad, or try to distract myself.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Like, in the past I’ve had times where I’d tell myself I should “get healthier” and I’d download some step-counting app or whatever, but then I’d forget about it after two days because my phone battery is already terrible and those apps always seem to drain it.\n\nBut as far as exercising to make up for eating… no, I don’t think I’ve done that. I mean, I’ve felt guilty and thought I should move more, but not like going to the gym for hours or punishing myself with workouts or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use any methods to try to compensate for the calories you consumed during these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really? So… no, I don’t think I really compensate like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever engage in any behaviors to try to prevent weight gain after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I worry about it, like I’ll stand there and be annoyed at myself, but I don’t really do a specific thing after to stop gaining weight.\n\nSometimes I’ll tell myself I’m going to “be good” the next day, but then the next day is just… work, and I’m scrubbing bathrooms or taking trash out and trying not to get bleach on my shoes. Or I’ll tell myself, okay, tomorrow I’ll get back on track, but then tomorrow I’m just tired and dealing with whatever’s going on at work. Like yesterday one of the soap dispensers leaked everywhere and it was this whole thing—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nBut no, I don’t think I really do anything to undo it.\n\nDoctor: Do you regularly have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short time without trying to make up for it afterwards?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that does happen.
